=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given two positive integers, l and r. A positive integer is called beautiful if the product of its digits is divisible by the sum of its digits.
Return the count of beautiful numbers between l and r, inclusive.
 
Example 1:

Input: l = 10, r = 20
Output: 2
Explanation:
The beautiful numbers in the range are 10 and 20.

Example 2:

Input: l = 1, r = 15
Output: 10
Explanation:
The beautiful numbers in the range are 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, and 10.

 
Constraints:

1 <= l <= r < 10^9

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def beautifulNumbers(self, l: int, r: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's draft code:

class Solution:
    def beautifulNumbers(self, l: int, r: int) -> int:
        from functools import lru_cache

        def count_up_to(n: int) -> int:
            if n <= 0:
                return 0
            digits = list(map(int, str(n)))
            L = len(digits)

            @lru_cache(None)
            def count_ways(pos: int, tight: bool) -> int:
                if pos == L:
                    return 1
                limit = digits[pos] if tight else 9
                total = 0
                for d in range(limit + 1):
                    total += count_ways(pos + 1, tight and d == limit)
                return total

            @lru_cache(None)
            def dfs(pos: int, tight: bool, started: bool, s: int, p: int) -> int:
                if pos == L:
                    return 1 if started and p % s == 0 else 0
                if started and p == 0:
                    return count_ways(pos, tight)
                limit = digits[pos] if tight else 9
                total = 0
                for d in range(limit + 1):
                    ntight = tight and (d == limit)  # careful: limit is digits[pos] if tight else 9. If tight false, ntight false. If tight true and d==digits[pos] true. But if tight false, limit=9, d==limit could be true when d=9, but ntight should remain false. So cannot use d==limit generally. Need ntight = tight and d == digits[pos] (not limit). Since if tight false, tight false. Use digits[pos].
                ...
            return dfs(0, True, False, 0, 1)

        return count_up_to(r) - count_up_to(l - 1)

Let's maybe write final code:

class Solution:
    def beautifulNumbers(self, l: int, r: int) -> int:
        from functools import lru_cache

        def count_up_to(n: int) -> int:
            if n <= 0:
                return 0
            digits = list(map(int, str(n)))
            L = len(digits)

            pow10 = [1] * (L + 1)
            for i in range(1, L + 1):
                pow10[i] = pow10[i - 1] * 10

            suffix = [0] * (L + 1)
            for i in range(L - 1, -1, -1):
                suffix[i] = digits[i] * pow10[L - 1 - i] + suffix[i + 1]

            def count_ways(pos: int, tight: bool) -> int:
                if pos == L:
                    return 1
                if tight:
                    return suffix[pos] + 1
                return pow10[L - pos]

            @lru_cache(None)
            def dfs(pos: int, tight: bool, started: bool, s: int, p: int) -> int:
                if pos == L:
                    return 1 if started and p % s == 0 else 0

                if started and p == 0:
                    return count_ways(pos, tight)

                limit = digits[pos] if tight else 9
                total = 0
                for d in range(limit + 1):
                    ntight = tight and (d == digits[pos])
                    if not started and d == 0:
                        total += dfs(pos + 1, ntight, False, 0, 1)
                    else:
                        total += dfs(pos + 1, ntight, True, s + d, p * d)
                return total

            return dfs(0, True, False, 0, 1)

        return count_up_to(r) - count_up_to(l - 1)

Need check `ntight = tight and (d == digits[pos])` when tight=False and d maybe equals digits[pos]? Since tight=False, ntight=False. Good.
But in the loop, `limit = digits[pos] if tight else 9`. If tight=False, d can be 0..9. `ntight = False and ...` = False. Good.
If tight=True, limit=digits[pos], d<=limit. `ntight = True and (d == digits[pos])`. Good.
